> **`OI-*` ID ALLOCATION — take the next id from YOUR LANE'S BLOCK. Never `max(existing)+1`.**
> Enforced by `whose_row.py --check-oi-ids`, which runs as a pre-commit check: a NEW id outside the
> committing identity's block fails the commit. **Blocks are matched to `git config user.name`**, so commit
> as `git -c user.name="Lane A (Eavail)"` — an unrecognised committer gets the `unattributed` block, never a
> free pass.
>
> | lane | block |
> |---|---|
> | *(pre-block era)* | `1-69` — **`1-65` allocated before this table existed; `66-69` ARE UNUSED AND CLOSED.** Do not backfill: `BEN-080`'s `047-059` lesson is that back-filling sorts a new item among old ones and silently breaks the one thing an id's ordering carries. |
> | A — Eavail | `70-79` |
> | B — uncertainty construction | `80-89` |
> | C — PET | `90-99` |
> | D — verifier | `100-109` |
> | repo infrastructure (ledgers, read path, dispatch machinery) | `110-119` |
> | Joseph / unattributed | `120-139` — the fallback, so a committer the table does not recognise is still *constrained* rather than exempt. **EXTENDED from `120-129` on 2026-08-17: that block is EXHAUSTED (`120`–`129` all in use) and the hook correctly refused `OI-130` as outside it.** The prescribed response is a free closed ten-block written into this table in the same commit — **appended to THIS row rather than given its own, because the hook keys the block off the COMMITTER NAME, and a second row naming a role rather than a committer is invisible to it.** *(A separate `personal-account mediator | 130-139` row was written first and rejected by the hook for exactly that reason; the rejection is recorded because the row looked correct to a human reader and was unreadable to the check.)* Freeness derived repo-wide, tracked and untracked, immediately before taking: `grep -rhoE 'OI-1[3-5][0-9]' --exclude-dir=.git .` returned only this commit's own `OI-130`. |
> | publication closeout | `140-149` — **SELF-ALLOCATED 2026-08-21** under the grant above, in the same commit as its first filing (`OI-140`..`OI-146`). The `120-139` fallback had only two ids free and seven rows were ready. **THE LANE NAME HERE IS LOAD-BEARING AND MUST STAY HYPHEN-FREE:** `whose_row._lane_key` splits on the first `[—\-/(]` and uppercases, so this cell and the committer's `user.name` must reduce to the SAME token. `publication closeout` -> `PUBLICATION CLOSEOUT` on both sides. A first attempt used the lane name `publication close-out` with committer `MINERvA-OmniFold agent (publication close-out)`: those reduce to `PUBLICATION CLOSE` and `MINERVA` respectively, matched nothing, and the hook correctly refused the whole filing. Commit into this block as `git -c user.name='publication closeout'`. |
> | publication closeout (2) | `150-159` — **SELF-ALLOCATED 2026-08-21** under the grant above, in the same commit as its first filing (`OI-150`), because `140-149` is EXHAUSTED (`140`–`149` all in use). Same committer `user.name` as the `140-149` row, `publication closeout`, and the same hyphen-free requirement applies: `whose_row._lane_key` reduces both the lane cell and the committer name by splitting on the first `[—\-/(]`, so both must reduce to `PUBLICATION CLOSEOUT`. The `(2)` suffix is AFTER the split point and therefore does not affect the key. |
> | *(unallocated)* | `160-169`, then `170-179`, … — **closed ten-blocks only** |

> **Why this exists, and it is not hypothetical.** `OI-*` had no block table and no addressing convention
> (`OI-62(b)`) while `BEN-*` had per-lane ranges. `max(existing)+1` was therefore the only available
> algorithm, and **two concurrent lanes running it collide by construction** — which is what happened on
> 2026-08-13: lane A and lane C independently allocated **both** `OI-64` and `OI-65`, four rows for two ids,
> surfaced only by a rebase. Lane A had filed the warning that morning (`BEN-223`). Writing the rule down
> did not work, which is why the table ships with a check rather than alone.
>
> **The two collided ids are NOT renumbered and are waived by id in `whose_row.OI_ID_WAIVERS`, dated and
> with reasons.** Both are cited in landed commits and sibling documents; renumbering would silently break
> those references (`BEN-216`/`BEN-219`). Each row instead leads with `⚠ ID COLLISION` naming the other's
> subject. **Reading either as one item is the `BEN-082` failure this guards against:** *"`OI-65` is open"*
> is currently true of both rows and means different work.
>
> **Self-allocation of a new block is granted**, mirroring Joseph's 2026-08-13 decision for `BEN-*`: take the
> next free **closed** ten-block and write it into this table **in the same commit as your first filing into
> it** — claimed by the first filing, never reserved ahead of one (lane C's phrasing, adopted).
>
> **STATUS: the table's SHAPE mirrors Joseph's existing `BEN-*` decision and is one commit to revert; it
> changes no existing id. `OI-62(b)` — whether `OI-*` should use lane blocks at all rather than lane-prefixed
> ids (`OI-A64`) — remains formally Joseph's call.** Landed 2026-08-14 on the mediator's instruction because
> the collision is live and a third instance was possible; see that row for what is and is not settled.

| OI-152 | OPEN | **UNOWNED** — shared-checkout working practice; two lanes hit the composing halves on 2026-08-21 | **A REPAIR WAS LOST WHILE BOTH LANES BELIEVED IT HAD LANDED, and neither habit is wrong on its own.** Reconstructed from the history rather than from either lane's recollection: `OI-144`'s row terminator was fixed in the working tree by its owner, then vanished, then was restored independently by this lane. Measured across commits, the row's pipe count went 8 at `a1854f62`, 7 at `c7dad0db`, 7 at `7dc61df6`, 8 at HEAD. **THE TWO MECHANISMS:** **(a) COPYING A SHARED FILE INTO A DETACHED WORKTREE CARRIES THE OTHER LANE'S UNCOMMITTED EDITS WITH IT.** Committing from a detached worktree is the sanctioned way to avoid sweeping a peer's files, and a `commit -F msg -- <paths>` pathspec protects the commit — but neither protects a file you `cp`'d wholesale first, because by then the peer's in-flight edit is in your copy. This lane did that. **(c) A PATHSPEC BOUNDS FILES, NOT AUTHORSHIP — AND YOUR OWN EDIT MASKS ANOTHER LANE'S IN A STATUS CHECK. MEASURED 2026-08-21, ON ME, AFTER THIS ROW WAS FILED.** `55c3a47d` was committed WITH an explicit pathspec and still carried **128 words of another lane's uncommitted prose** into the `OI-146` row — a paragraph recording that `\nwSystResid` / `\nwStatResid` had been un-struck by parent propagation. A pathspec commits the WHOLE FILE at that path, including edits inside it that are not yours, so the usual advice *"use a pathspec"* does not prevent this and I was already following it. **The check that would have caught it is a ROW-BY-ROW DIFF AGAINST HEAD before committing a shared file, confirming every changed row is one you touched** — I ran exactly that before `fa36d13f` and skipped it before `55c3a47d`. The reason I skipped it is the sharp part: `git status` showed `M docs/OPEN_ITEMS.md` and I attributed that entirely to myself BECAUSE I HAD JUST EDITED THAT FILE. **A lane's own change masks a peer's in any signal that reports the FILE rather than the HUNK.** Found and reported by the negweight durability lane, whose content it was; the paragraph is correct and stays, and this clause exists so its authorship is not misread as mine. **(b) AN EMPTY `git status` AFTER SOMEONE ELSE'S HEAD MOVES IS NOT EVIDENCE YOUR CHANGE LANDED — IT IS EQUALLY EVIDENCE IT WAS OVERWRITTEN.** The `OI-144` lane read a clean status as confirmation; what had happened is that the primary checkout was moved to another lane's HEAD and its uncommitted repair was gone, so there was nothing left to differ. Both readings produce an identical clean status and only one is true. That lane's own words: it had written the *read the regenerated row, not the exit code* rule an hour earlier and then failed the same shape one level up. | **Write both into the shared working practice rather than leaving them as two lanes' scar tissue.** The check that separates the two readings of a clean status is the only one that works: **read the content AT THE COMMIT** (`git show <sha>:<path>`), never the diff against a moved HEAD. And when committing from a worktree, apply the edit inside the worktree or diff the copied file row-by-row against HEAD first — this lane did the latter on its next commit and it caught nothing only because there was nothing to catch by then. | No science impact: the lost repair was a table terminator, and it is restored. Filed for the MECHANISM, because the same two habits applied to a receipt, a pinned constant or a state cell would lose something that matters, and both lanes were following a rule they had themselves written. Also settled here, so it is not re-chased: `OI-124` is NOT malformed — it splits to 6 only under a stricter space-delimited separator because one cell ends without a space before its pipe, and its field count is 7. The genuine exceptions are FOUR rows, not three as this lane wrote twice while correctly enumerating four: `OI-130` at 5, `OI-30` and `OI-62` at 8, `OI-125` at 11. See `OI-148`. | 2026-08-21 |

The floor is still the same order as the spread and still cannot be the whole of it. The subtraction cannot be done yet — `central_vector` sums to 1 in the training artifacts and the total is not measurable there — and B is running the extraction stage to make it possible. **So: OPEN and NARROWING, not open and idle.** The question to answer once the subtraction lands is whether the residual beyond Poisson-plus-floor is large enough to demand an explanation, and only then does it bear on **`niter = 3`**, which `OPEN_ITEMS` items (d)/(e) already record as owing a bias-variance justification rather than a gate-behaviour one. This couples directly to the **`niter = 3`** choice, which `OPEN_ITEMS` items (d)/(e) already record as owing a **bias-variance** justification rather than a gate-behaviour one — a regularization parameter set from closure passing is exactly what an amplification factor would expose. **Does not block construction**, and `C_stat` is correctly named regardless. ~~Cheapest first step is `CSTAT-O2a`, which bounds the non-determinism floor and so bounds how much of the 4.478% is not the draw.~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 by [`RULING-20260817-lanec-floor-vs-family-coherence.md`](https://github.com/josephbaileyy/MINERvA-OmniFold/blob/0b329e8ae8482e6334a68faf947fc80ae7265ac9/docs/orchestration/RULING-20260817-lanec-floor-vs-family-coherence.md "evidence/prepublication-2026-08-20-0b329e8a:docs/orchestration/RULING-20260817-lanec-floor-vs-family-coherence.md"), which is cheaper still: `CSTAT-O2a` need not run, because the subtraction is INSENSITIVE to the floor.** Measured on the 63-cell tail, as a **bracket over the floor-vs-residual correlation** rather than as an sd ratio — because quadrature assumes an independence `VL130` says `n=4` cannot test: **the residual is `58.5%`–`75.7%` for any `ρ`, and the floor is `11%`–`15%` of it throughout** (`ρ=0` → the `1.9%` variance share; `ρ=+1` → `12.83%`, which is exactly the reciprocal of the sd ratio and so the **maximum-correlation bound**). Only a **4× understated floor AND `ρ ≈ +1` together** would overturn it. **So `CSTAT-O2a`'s own predeclared trigger — *"only if the subtraction proves sensitive to that difference"* — does NOT fire, and it stays HELD.** The ruling also disqualifies the `7.80×`/`7.18×` sd ratio from being quoted at all (the floor is on `central_vector`, shape-only and **understating**, against the family's `xsec`; `±35.36%` on the denominator at `n=5`; and `FLOOR_INTERMEDIATE` is `VL128`'s verdict on a different functional, not `VL130`'s). **The finding survives on the variance share, not on the ratio: the floor is not the explanation for the tail spread.** | [SPEC §8](orchestration/SPEC-20260814-gate5-cstat-construction-v1.md), [BEN-232](https://github.com/josephbaileyy/MINERvA-OmniFold/blob/0b329e8ae8482e6334a68faf947fc80ae7265ac9/docs/orchestration/FINDING-20260814-ninety-times-counting-statistics.md "evidence/prepublication-2026-08-20-0b329e8a:docs/orchestration/FINDING-20260814-ninety-times-counting-statistics.md"), [ruling](https://github.com/josephbaileyy/MINERvA-OmniFold/blob/0b329e8ae8482e6334a68faf947fc80ae7265ac9/docs/orchestration/RULING-20260817-lanec-floor-vs-family-coherence.md "evidence/prepublication-2026-08-20-0b329e8a:docs/orchestration/RULING-20260817-lanec-floor-vs-family-coherence.md") | 2026-08-17 |

Green says the receipts still POINT where they pointed and says nothing about whether the artifacts changed, the same disclaimer `BEN-325` makes about the count. **SEPARATE AND NOT ACTED ON — the hook's own UNLOCK TRIGGER, WHOSE VALUE FLIPPED THREE TIMES TODAY AND SHOULD NOT BE HONOURED AS WRITTEN.** **First, the correction, because I got this wrong in the direction that matters: I reported the trigger FIRED and it was false within the hour, broken by my own commit** — `8a34f12^` exit 0, **`8a34f12` exit 1** (that commit added the OI-96 pin generator and its tests, both mentioning the namespace, so **the fix for this very item broke the trigger I was reporting on**), `be06d45` shipped a hook paragraph asserting it had fired, false on the tree carrying it. **Caught by lane A with a parent-commit control, not by me** (`BEN-394`). Restored to exit 0 by dispositioning my five occurrences as `# NS-EXEMPT`, and every reading in the hook header is now bound to a sha. **The flip-flop is a reason, not an embarrassment — the predicate is tree-global, any lane flips it either way, and nothing announces either transition.** **My second objection survives a fixed tree and that is what decides it:** `.githooks/pre-commit`'s header says *"when `check_canonical_designation.py` exits 0 on a clean tree, move this to the run list as check 7 [now 10]. No other condition, no judgement call — run the command."* Measured today: **it exits 0.** But wiring it would install a check that goes RED when a committer deletes a prose sentence, which fails the dispatcher's OWN admitting rule at `:11`. **The trigger tests the wrong property** — "green today" is not "a committer who did nothing wrong can always make it pass" — and it is the hook owner's call, not mine. **RULED 2026-08-17 by the hook owner: DO NOT WIRE IT.** The measurement and the ruling are now written into `.githooks/pre-commit`'s header **beside the trigger itself**, because a reader of the hook would otherwise see a fired trigger and assume it had been missed rather than declined — the header's own *"an unlisted check is not a decision"* rule, applied to a trigger. Hook behaviour unchanged: still 9 `run` lines, still `9 checks passed`. **The general form, recorded there because it outlives this item: a trigger written to REMOVE judgement is precisely the one place nobody re-derives the property — automating a decision does not verify the predicate it automates, it guarantees the predicate stops being checked.** | [`BEN-237`](https://github.com/josephbaileyy/MINERvA-OmniFold/blob/0b329e8ae8482e6334a68faf947fc80ae7265ac9/docs/orchestration/FINDING-20260815-a-consumer-is-not-a-file-extension.md "evidence/prepublication-2026-08-20-0b329e8a:docs/orchestration/FINDING-20260815-a-consumer-is-not-a-file-extension.md"), `OI-81`, `BEN-322`, `BEN-387` | 2026-08-17 |
